per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect
produces a volume factor. Uniformity follows from the rooted envelope and the finite local template
atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

The six one-step maps.          The exact regular step is organized in the following order.
(E1) Normal chart return. Substitute the finite-volume analytic normal section into the local Wilson,
     Haar, inherited, and source factors.


                                                              34
(E2) Coarea logarithm. Append the field-dependent logarithm

                                                  − log det(I + DN P ),

     with the fixed linear determinant owned once outside the activity map.
(E3) Summed connected logarithm. Apply the simultaneous hard-core/Gaussian replica-BKAR
     identity and sum the exact 1/n! series.
(E4) Local projection. Extract the complete gauge-covariant Wick-local jet and retain the short Taylor
     remainder.
(E5) Canonical hull and collect. Report covariance paths and finite collars, assign each history to
     one canonical hull, and collect exactly once.
(E6) Output recertification. Derive the next regulator allocation from the actual post-localization
     output gradient and complex Hessian.

Lemma 4.24 (One common support exponent). Suppose the covariance connector has decay exponent
µ > 0, and the normal chart, inverse trees, coarea logarithm, and fixed local atlas have positive exponential

                                      mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas > 0.

                                0 < κ < min{µ, mchart , minv , mcoa , matlas }.

Proof. For a BKAR tree, report every covariance edge by a shortest path and every hard-core edge by its
fixed collar. The geometric reporting inequality gives

and atlas branch is summable at exponent κ. Localization costs a polynomial in the rooted radius but
does not alter the exponent. Canonical collect is a partition of histories, so it does not duplicate a support
weight.

Lemma 4.25 (One common analytic species tube). There is a radius ηcom > 0, independent of volume
and preceding weak depth, on which the Wilson word, exponential-coordinate Haar density, Karcher
normal return, coarea logarithm, and finite Schur endpoint are simultaneously holomorphic. On the closed
half-radius tube, their derivatives through structural order three and every fixed passive-source order have
finite fixed-template suprema.

Proof. Each species is a finite composition of multiplication, inversion, exponential, logarithm, the
finite-cell Karcher mean, and an inverse of one of the matrices

                                         I + DN P,          Hopt + B(A).

The path-atlas theorem supplies a positive holomorphy radius for the first five maps. The uniform
inequalities ∥DN P ∥ < 1 and Hopt ≥ 2I persist on a smaller positive tube, so the two inverses are
holomorphic there by a Neumann series. There are only finitely many bulk and aligned-reflection
templates. Taking the minimum of their radii gives ηcom > 0; Cauchy’s estimate on the closed half-radius
tube gives the uniform derivative constants.
   For each fixed local template define the finite constants CW , CHaar , Ccoa , Cchart , CSch as the suprema
on that closed common tube of the third derivatives of respectively the Wilson word, Haar logarithm,
coarea logarithm, normal return, and finite Schur endpoint after their scalar and complete two-jet parts
have been removed. Let Cinc be the largest rooted incidence number of those templates and let Cconn be
the row sum of the exponential covariance connector of Corollary 4.14. All these constants are finite and
uniform under the standing convention.

2.2    Exact normal fiber chart and coarea
Let Xm , Ym be exponentially weighted Banach coordinate spaces and let

                    L : Xm → Ym ,         P : Ym → Xm ,              LP = I,        ∥P ∥m = p < 1.

Theorem 2.5 (Normal fiber chart). For w ∈ Ym and ζ ∈ K, consider
                                       F (y; w, ζ) = y + N (P y + ζ) − w.
Assume on a product ball that
                                       η := sup ∥DN (P y + ζ)P ∥m < 1.
Then there is a unique analytic solution y = T (w, ζ) and
                                            PN (w, ζ) := P T (w, ζ) + ζ
is an exact fiber chart:
                                                  B(PN (w, ζ)) = w.
If A = I + DN (PN )P , then
                                Dw T = A− 1,            Dζ T = −A− 1DN (PN )|K ,
and hence
                           Dw PN = P A− 1,           Dζ PN = IK − P A− 1DN (PN )|K .
All derivatives through order three are finite sums of products of A− 1, P, D2 N, D3 N and are exponentially
local in the same Banach matrix algebra.
Proof. For fixed (w, ζ), the map
                                                 y 7→ w − N (P y + ζ)
is an η-contraction on a sufficiently chosen invariant ball. Banach’s fixed-point theorem gives T . The
exact fiber identity follows by substitution. Differentiating
                                              T + N (P T + ζ) = w
gives the displayed first derivatives. Repeated differentiation gives the second- and third-order Bell-
polynomial formulas. Since ∥A− 1∥ ≤ (1 − η)− 1, every term is bounded in the same exponential matrix
algebra.

2.3     Nonlinear forty-nine-path section
Lemma 2.8 (Finite-cell analytic atlas and uniform Cauchy bounds). Let P49 be the finite path atlas
for one retained cell and its finite aligned-reflection template family. There exist fixed radii 0 < η0 < η1 ,
uniform under the standing convention, such that the product exponential, multiplication, inversion,
logarithm, and Karcher-mean maps defining every template extend holomorphically from Tη0 to Tη1 .
   Write the canonically rescaled local block as

                             B(x) = Lx + N (x),       N (0) = 0,             DN (0) = 0.

For every exponential kernel rate 0 < m < mP and r = 2, 3, there is a finite constant

                                             CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ) < ∞

such that
                                    sup ∥Dr N (x)∥Km ≤ CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ),
                                   x∈Tη0

uniformly under the standing convention. The same constants, enlarged by a finite maximum, serve the
aligned-reflection templates.
Proof. For one retained cell the block depends on finitely many g coordinates. The exponential and
multiplication maps are entire, while inversion and the principal logarithm are holomorphic on a fixed
neighborhood of the identity. At the flat field the Karcher critical point has positive Hessian on the
retained tangent space; the analytic implicit-function theorem therefore supplies a unique holomorphic
Karcher mean on a fixed complex neighborhood. The path family and the reflected template family are
finite, so one may choose common nested radii 0 < η0 < η1 for all of them. On the larger finite-dimensional
complex polydisc, every elementary map and their finite compositions are holomorphic and uniformly
bounded. Cauchy’s integral estimate on the smaller polydisc gives uniform derivative bounds through
order three with denominator powers of η1 − η0 .
   The global block is obtained by translating this fixed finite cell. Each output row meets only a fixed
number of input cells, and each input column enters only a fixed number of output cells. Multiplying the
finite-cell Cauchy bounds by these fixed incidence numbers gives the two-sided exponentially weighted
Schur-kernel bounds. These incidence numbers do not depend on the total volume or preceding weak
depth. The aligned-reflection family is finite, so one takes a finite maximum.

Put g = u and Rg = g −2ε with 0 < ε < 1/6. The symbol χ > 0 denotes the curvature-bad threshold

                                     X                              X

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of

Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),

             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of
the forty-nine path inputs. It vanishes on the flat set, its small sublevel set lies inside the common Karcher
chart, and it uses only one fixed enlarged-cell template. On that sublevel set the retained holonomies
Wp′ are defined by the local Karcher atlas. Multiply the displayed retained deficit by a fixed chart cutoff
that is one on a prescribed smaller Karcher sublevel and zero before the edge of the larger Karcher tube;
this gives a smooth invariant extension to the complete finite-cell configuration space. The threshold tch
below is chosen so that the entire curvature-label support lies where this cutoff equals one.
Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

of the flat set.

minimum and the smoothly extended DQ is bounded, so their quotient is bounded there as well. This

bulk and aligned-reflection templates proves the stated uniformity.

uniform under the standing convention, such that

For a pivot product GI , tensorization gives the constant CG,sG . The original-support reserve absorbs its

                       Group               dim G      sG        coefficient 11CA log 2/(24π 2 )
                       SU(4)                 15       4                    11 log 2/(6π 2 )

   The higher Sobolev order concerns internal compact-pivot derivatives. It does not increase the physical
source-mark budget; those marks remain the fixed orders consumed by the boundary-to-center and Ward
arguments. In the ordered-multijet realization of Lemma A.17, one Sobolev monomial uses at most
eight distinct flow variables at an SU(4) or G2 pivot and at most six at an Sp(2) pivot. Repeated color
directions retain distinct variables. Thus, for example, D12 D22 is represented by the ordered word (1, 1, 2, 2)
rather than by a fourth derivative along a single flow.

D      Ordinary periodic torons, seams, and wrapping templates
The finite-volume model used throughout is the ordinary periodic product space GE(Λ) with periodic
gauge transformations. It is a single, explicit lattice model; no twisted boundary condition and no sum
over nontrivial principal-bundle sectors is part of the construction. Since the target spacetime R4 is
contractible, this restriction is compatible with the theorem’s local continuum scope.
  Define the compact set of ordinary periodic flat holonomies

                          TG := {(h1 , h2 , h3 , h4 ) ∈ G4 : [hµ , hν ] = e for all µ, ν}.

Lemma D.1 (Tree-gauge reduction of ordinary periodic flat fields). Every flat configuration in the
ordinary periodic link model is gauge equivalent to a seam representative whose interior links are the
identity and whose four fundamental seam holonomies form an element of TG . Conversely every element
of TG defines such a flat periodic configuration. The remaining gauge action is simultaneous conjugation
of the four holonomies.


                                                          110
Proof. Choose a maximal lattice tree in a fundamental periodic cell and gauge its links to the identity.
Flatness makes the holonomy of a path depend only on its homotopy class. The four links closing the
fundamental cycles therefore supply holonomies hµ . The commutator loop of two fundamental cycles
is a product of plaquette boundaries, so flatness gives [hµ , hν ] = e. Reversing this construction gives
a flat seam representative from every commuting four-tuple. A gauge transformation at the tree root
conjugates all four holonomies simultaneously, and all other gauge freedom has already been fixed.
Lemma D.2 (Finite toron and seam atlas). For every fixed compact G, the set TG has a finite cover by
ambient product-coordinate neighborhoods on which every block word, Karcher word, coarea factor, Haar
factor, reflected word, and source word used in the proof is analytic in the local variables and in the toron
parameters. All derivatives through order 2sG + 3 have a finite common bound. The aligned coordinate
reflections and seam placements add only a finite template family.
Proof. The commutator equations define a closed subset of compact G4 , hence TG is compact. Around
each of its points choose ordinary product charts in the ambient manifold G4 ; no quotient chart or
classification of singular conjugacy classes is required. In tree gauge, every local factor is a fixed finite
composition of multiplication, inversion, the local exponential/logarithm, and the Karcher implicit
function, with some arguments left- or right-multiplied by the fixed seam holonomies. These maps are
analytic on a sufficiently small product neighborhood and have finite Cauchy bounds through the declared
order. Compactness gives a finite subcover. There are four seam directions and a finite aligned-reflection
family, so taking a finite maximum gives the common constants.
Theorem D.3 (Ordinary-periodic toron and optical admission). The complete full-alias right-inverse,
normal-section, optical-coercivity, Haar-minorization, owner, and boundary-to-center estimates hold
uniformly over every flat toron background in TG , every admitted seam and aligned-reflection template,
and every periodic wrapping certificate. The acoustic toron/background variables remain explicit and are
never inverted. No constant uniform over different groups is asserted.
Proof. Lift a seam representative to the universal covering lattice. A flat background is gauged to the
identity there; deck translation in direction µ acts on Lie-algebra fields by the unitary operator Ad(hµ ).
Since the hµ commute, these unitary operators are simultaneously diagonalizable over the complexified
color space. On each joint color eigenspace the folded block and optical symbols are therefore the
identity-background, color-free symbols evaluated at a real quasi-momentum shifted by the corresponding
deck phase. The estimates
                                                  49                      8
                                Af (k)Af (k)∗ ⪰      I4 ,   ∥P0 (k)∥2 ≤      ,
                                                   2                      49
and the printed complex-strip and optical lower bounds hold for every real quasi-momentum. They
consequently survive every toron phase without a loss of constant. The retained acoustic variables,
including toron tangents, are kept in the exterior/background register; only the same normal optical
complement is inverted.
   Lemma D.2 supplies the nonlinear, coarea, Haar, and marked derivative bounds uniformly over a finite
toron/seam cover. On the covering lattice the local gauge trivialization sends every plaquette holonomy
to a conjugate of the corresponding fluctuation plaquette. The representation-normalized Wilson function
is conjugation invariant, so the charge and its differentiated finite-word ledger are unchanged by this
trivialization. The 2sG differentiated bad-core row of Theorem 3.20 is therefore uniform on the same
cover. Consequently the complete one-pivot score and Haar minorization retain their printed constants
after taking the finite template maximum. On intersections use the fixed least-label owner, so no factor is
duplicated. A wrapping certificate records only the four lattice directions, the fixed block range, a finite
seam crossing pattern, and an aligned reflection type; there are finitely many such local templates at one
normalized step. Their iteration is already controlled by the permanent wrapping ideal rather than by a
sum over topological sectors. Taking the finite maximum proves the stated admission and leaves the joint
Haar–optical boundary-to-center mechanism unchanged.

E     Invariant tensor spaces through engineering dimension six
The low-dimensional color input can be stated without choosing a basis. The degree-two invariant
polynomial is unique for every simple g. A primitive degree-three invariant occurs only for Ar , r ≥ 2.
Hence the reflection-even dimension-four scalar block is one-dimensional for every G, while the dimension-
six block may contain the additional cubic curvature contraction in type A. The antisymmetric structure


                                                      111
tensor is always present through the bracket but contributes only inside the already retained covariant
derivative and commutator jets.
   The manuscript uses the complete finite quotient generated by curvature, covariant derivatives, metric
and epsilon contractions, invariant color tensors, total-difference classes, and equation-of-motion contacts
through the declared engineering cutoff. No argument relies on a hand-picked SU(2) basis once this
quotient is used.

F      Theorem-level specialization and dependency map
The purpose of this appendix is provenance rather than a new estimate. It identifies every independent
fixed-group input in the register CG , the first proof call at which that dependence enters, and the later
statements that consume it. In particular, the completion proof in Theorem 11.1 does not rely on an
unprinted constant or on a rank-one identity.

1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
1.1    Problem and result
The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
   Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
                                                        1
                                                                    
                                   wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
                                                       dρ
where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
   The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
                                              H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
                                            11 CA (G) log 2
                           u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
                                                 24π 2
with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
                                              dG                    dG
                                      sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
                                               4                      2
The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
but its constants may depend on CG .
Proposition 1.2 (Reflection-positive Wilson input). For every finite periodic lattice Λ and u > 0, in the
ordinary periodic link sector,
                                                "                    #

is gauge invariant and reflection positive for coordinate reflections. Moreover wG,ρ ≥ 0, its zero set is the

                                     cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2 .

Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
therefore applies.

1.3     Proof architecture
The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
   Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
                                                                                       Q
the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
   Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
   Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.

Only the theorem-level inputs in Table 1 are imported. The fixed-group geometry, action charge, higher
Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
noncollapse source are internal.

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber
contraction are proved in the later normal-chart chapters.
Proposition 2.3 (Exact coarea–fiber identity). At finite volume, in the normal fiber chart,

                                    dU = J0 det(I + DN P )−1 dW dζ,

2.3     Nonlinear forty-nine-path section
Lemma 2.8 (Finite-cell analytic atlas and uniform Cauchy bounds). Let P49 be the finite path atlas
for one retained cell and its finite aligned-reflection template family. There exist fixed radii 0 < η0 < η1 ,
uniform under the standing convention, such that the product exponential, multiplication, inversion,
logarithm, and Karcher-mean maps defining every template extend holomorphically from Tη0 to Tη1 .
   Write the canonically rescaled local block as

                             B(x) = Lx + N (x),       N (0) = 0,             DN (0) = 0.

For every exponential kernel rate 0 < m < mP and r = 2, 3, there is a finite constant

                                             CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ) < ∞

such that
                                    sup ∥Dr N (x)∥Km ≤ CN,r (m; η0 , η1 ),
                                   x∈Tη0

uniformly under the standing convention. The same constants, enlarged by a finite maximum, serve the
aligned-reflection templates.
Proof. For one retained cell the block depends on finitely many g coordinates. The exponential and
multiplication maps are entire, while inversion and the principal logarithm are holomorphic on a fixed
neighborhood of the identity. At the flat field the Karcher critical point has positive Hessian on the
retained tangent space; the analytic implicit-function theorem therefore supplies a unique holomorphic
Karcher mean on a fixed complex neighborhood. The path family and the reflected template family are
finite, so one may choose common nested radii 0 < η0 < η1 for all of them. On the larger finite-dimensional
complex polydisc, every elementary map and their finite compositions are holomorphic and uniformly
bounded. Cauchy’s integral estimate on the smaller polydisc gives uniform derivative bounds through
order three with denominator powers of η1 − η0 .
   The global block is obtained by translating this fixed finite cell. Each output row meets only a fixed
number of input cells, and each input column enters only a fixed number of output cells. Multiplying the
finite-cell Cauchy bounds by these fixed incidence numbers gives the two-sided exponentially weighted
Schur-kernel bounds. These incidence numbers do not depend on the total volume or preceding weak
depth. The aligned-reflection family is finite, so one takes a finite maximum.

Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

5       Invariant graph and universal coefficient
The marginal coordinate is u = g 2 . Inputs SI–6, SI–6B, SI–7, and SI–7M are used only to compute
the local perturbative Taylor coefficient of the complete fine Wilson vector-minus-ghost quotient. The
coefficient of the exact Karcher map is identified internally by evaluating one local standard background
coordinate on the two equal measures related by the exact Karcher pushforward, followed by unit-tangent
conjugacy on the centered invariant family. No unrestricted Haar-translated background functional, global
background-gauge section, or application of Reisz power counting to the optical conditional covariance is
consumed.

printed after the centered invariant graph, where exact measure equality and parabolic conjugacy identify
the one-step coefficient.

The coefficient is extracted from a local background renormalization condition on the full weak measure.
The background is not inserted as a freely translatable factor in an unrestricted Haar integral, and no
global gauge section or equality of globally gauge-fixed partition functions is asserted.

Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
    (i) cutting every carrier at U (F) ∪ Rg (F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
   (ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
        incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
        possible side branches;
  (iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
        cost on U (F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
        magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg (F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution

  (iv) the no-crossing terminal remainder tends to zero through two g-derivatives uniformly as M → ∞.

Lemma 10.3 (Existence of the graph-segment interpolation). After choosing r in the common weak
corridor, the segment 0 ≤ g ≤ gr lies in the same real marked domain. At finite cutoff and volume,
evaluate the exact interaction at (g 2 , R⋆ (g 2 )) and express its quadratic part relative to the fixed g = 0
grounded Gaussian. This gives a real C 2 family Vg,r with positive finite normalization. In fixed Gaussian
coordinates its complete local expansion has the exact grading
                           X
                   Vg,r =      g ν Vn,ν;r ,     ν ≥ max{n − 2, 0},       ν ≡ n (mod 2).                   (171)
                            n,ν

Here n is field degree. The Wilson row attains the lower exponent ν = n − 2; Haar, chart, coarea,
determinant, and graph-return rows carry additional nonnegative powers. The grading is read only after
each complete BRS/reflection packet is collected. The graph is C 3 in u, so g 7→ (g 2 , R⋆ (g 2 )) is C 2 at
g = 0. The Wilson word, Haar density, normal return, coarea, determinant, and finite Schur endpoint are
analytic in the physical field A = ga. After the Gaussian quadratic form is removed, a Wilson term of field
degree n carries g n−2 ; species without the g −2 Wilson prefactor carry higher powers. Analyticity under
A = ga and the even dependence of the graph coefficients on g give the congruence in (171). Gauge/ghost
partners and coordinate Jacobians are collected before the grading is read, so BRS and reflection relations
are retained. No C 1 statement in the squared coupling u is used. Finite-volume Gaussian equivalence and
the common analytic tube give positivity, integrability, and uniform C 2 control in g.

Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the
complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties.
    (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF , and F F rows of orders two,
        three, and four. After the exact global contact is subtracted, every complete reflection/BRS packet
        has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi )2 at weak scale i.
   (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
        derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
        differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
        cancellation assumption.
 (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
        in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
        lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
        row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
  (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
        cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
        its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
        zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.
Consequently the complete normalized two-root functional, with or without one fixed dimension-six defect
root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .
Proof. Appendix A gives the full proof inside this manuscript. Its five subsections establish the grounded-
torus covariance and globally subtracted packet division; the weak crossing recurrence and wrapping rows;
the twice-marked conditional-packet contraction; the pre-owner hybrid-polydisc Wiener and compact-
entrance estimates; and the terminal-shell summation. No additional mathematical input is imported
beyond the results proved in Sections 3–7.

Compatibility of the physical scale with the common diagonal. Let f be the profile of

Before taking the common diagonal subsequence, enlarge the countable source algebra by the fixed fields
Yr (f ), ΘYr (f ), and their reflected product. The same single diagonal extraction then gives all Schwinger
limits entering their OS form and preserves every cutoff- and volume-uniform lower bound. The scale r is
not changed after extraction. The cofinal dyadic choice is countable, while u(r) → 0 and r2 L(r)pf → 0
along it. Hence one r satisfies the displayed inequality. Adding finitely many fields at that scale preserves
countability of the source algebra. The source-tree theorem and Corollary 8.12 therefore permit the
same diagonal argument, and convergence of the finitely many Schwinger functions passes a uniform
finite-cutoff lower bound to the limit.

positive-time reflected local curvature functional. Suppose along the commo

Proof. Work first at finite directed depth M and inside one crossing cell of Definition A.6. Apply the
exact marked conditional forest. If the top marked ancestor is a weak conditional vertex of scale j,
Lemma A.14 gives the complete row at that scale. Every later weak transfer before the selected terminal
factor is unmarked, and Theorem A.13 contracts the entire complete rooted packet in one norm. A
coupling mark after the two source roots have joined is not omitted: it becomes another distinguished
leaf and moves the top marked ancestor to its own scale.
   If the top marked ancestor is the selected terminal factor, apply Theorem A.22. On a crossing cell
this is the complete compact handoff packet, with the two differences created at the first-crossing scale
and the remaining separation paid by the joint Haar–optical connector. On the no-crossing cell it is
the finite-depth weak terminal packet on the fixed coarse torus. The latter tends to zero through two
g derivatives as M → ∞. Thus no bridge-free expansion of the residual grounded covariance and no
massless Schur row is required.

                               j using (213) and add the uniform terminal row (240). The result is a
polynomial times 2−2k eC k , independent of M and of the crossing cell. The sum of the shell bounds
is uniform up to the closure of each crossing cell. The cellwise shell series and its first two derivatives
therefore pass to the cutoff and volume limits uniformly on that closed cell. At a shared boundary the
original total finite-volume normalized functional is C 2 by Lemma 10.3; equality of the two one-sided
total derivatives passes to the limit. Thus no individual shell coefficients are compared and the crossing
index is never differentiated. At g = 0 use the no-crossing cell for every M and let M → ∞.
   The shell majorant is summable in k. Dominated convergence therefore permits the directed-depth,
volume and ultraviolet limits and two g derivatives, also with one fixed defect root.

   For an arbitrary compact connected simple group G = G/Γ e G , where ΓG ⊂ Z(G),e choose any faithful
unitary representation and the scalar cρ in Section 1. The Lie-algebraic block and Karcher constants
are unchanged under an isogeny once the metric is fixed. The chosen faithful representation can change
the higher Wilson-action constants, but they remain finite. The actual compact link group G enters the
ordinary-periodic toron set of Appendix D; no separate twist-sector sum is used.

                       Group               dim G      sG        coefficient 11CA log 2/(24π 2 )

   The higher Sobolev order concerns internal compact-pivot derivatives. It does not increase the physical
source-mark budget; those marks remain the fixed orders consumed by the boundary-to-center and Ward
arguments. In the ordered-multijet realization of Lemma A.17, one Sobolev monomial uses at most
eight distinct flow variables at an SU(4) or G2 pivot and at most six at an Sp(2) pivot. Repeated color
directions retain distinct variables. Thus, for example, D12 D22 is represented by the ordered word (1, 1, 2, 2)
rather than by a fourth derivative along a single flow.

D      Ordinary periodic torons, seams, and wrapping templates
The finite-volume model used throughout is the ordinary periodic product space GE(Λ) with periodic
gauge transformations. It is a single, explicit lattice model; no twisted boundary condition and no sum
over nontrivial principal-bundle sectors is part of the construction. Since the target spacetime R4 is
contractible, this restriction is compatible with the theorem’s local continuum scope.
  Define the compact set of ordinary periodic flat holonomies

                          TG := {(h1 , h2 , h3 , h4 ) ∈ G4 : [hµ , hν ] = e for all µ, ν}.

Lemma D.1 (Tree-gauge reduction of ordinary periodic flat fields). Every flat configuration in the
ordinary periodic link model is gauge equivalent to a seam representative whose interior links are the
identity and whose four fundamental seam holonomies form an element of TG . Conversely every element
of TG defines such a flat periodic configuration. The remaining gauge action is simultaneous conjugation
of the four holonomies.


                                                          110
Proof. Choose a maximal lattice tree in a fundamental periodic cell and gauge its links to the identity.
Flatness makes the holonomy of a path depend only on its homotopy class. The four links closing the
fundamental cycles therefore supply holonomies hµ . The commutator loop of two fundamental cycles
is a product of plaquette boundaries, so flatness gives [hµ , hν ] = e. Reversing this construction gives
a flat seam representative from every commuting four-tuple. A gauge transformation at the tree root
conjugates all four holonomies simultaneously, and all other gauge freedom has already been fixed.
Lemma D.2 (Finite toron and seam atlas). For every fixed compact G, the set TG has a finite cover by
ambient product-coordinate neighborhoods on which every block word, Karcher word, coarea factor, Haar
factor, reflected word, and source word used in the proof is analytic in the local variables and in the toron
parameters. All derivatives through order 2sG + 3 have a finite common bound. The aligned coordinate
reflections and seam placements add only a finite template family.
Proof. The commutator equations define a closed subset of compact G4 , hence TG is compact. Around
each of its points choose ordinary product charts in the ambient manifold G4 ; no quotient chart or
classification of singular conjugacy classes is required. In tree gauge, every local factor is a fixed finite
composition of multiplication, inversion, the local exponential/logarithm, and the Karcher implicit
function, with some arguments left- or right-multiplied by the fixed seam holonomies. These maps are
analytic on a sufficiently small product neighborhood and have finite Cauchy bounds through the declared
order. Compactness gives a finite subcover. There are four seam directions and a finite aligned-reflection
family, so taking a finite maximum gives the common constants.
Theorem D.3 (Ordinary-periodic toron and optical admission). The complete full-alias right-inverse,
normal-section, optical-coercivity, Haar-minorization, owner, and boundary-to-center estimates hold
uniformly over every flat toron background in TG , every admitted seam and aligned-reflection template,
and every periodic wrapping certificate. The acoustic toron/background variables remain explicit and are
never inverted. No constant uniform over different groups is asserted.
Proof. Lift a seam representative to the universal covering lattice. A flat background is gauged to the
identity there; deck translation in direction µ acts on Lie-algebra fields by the unitary operator Ad(hµ ).
Since the hµ commute, these unitary operators are simultaneously diagonalizable over the complexified
color space. On each joint color eigenspace the folded block and optical symbols are therefore the
identity-background, color-free symbols evaluated at a real quasi-momentum shifted by the corresponding
deck phase. The estimates
                                                  49                      8
                                Af (k)Af (k)∗ ⪰      I4 ,   ∥P0 (k)∥2 ≤      ,
                                                   2                      49
and the printed complex-strip and optical lower bounds hold for every real quasi-momentum. They
consequently survive every toron phase without a loss of constant. The retained acoustic variables,
including toron tangents, are kept in the exterior/background register; only the same normal optical
complement is inverted.
   Lemma D.2 supplies the nonlinear, coarea, Haar, and marked derivative bounds uniformly over a finite
toron/seam cover. On the covering lattice the local gauge trivialization sends every plaquette holonomy
to a conjugate of the corresponding fluctuation plaquette. The representation-normalized Wilson function
is conjugation invariant, so the charge and its differentiated finite-word ledger are unchanged by this
trivialization. The 2sG differentiated bad-core row of Theorem 3.20 is therefore uniform on the same
cover. Consequently the complete one-pivot score and Haar minorization retain their printed constants
after taking the finite template maximum. On intersections use the fixed least-label owner, so no factor is
duplicated. A wrapping certificate records only the four lattice directions, the fixed block range, a finite
seam crossing pattern, and an aligned reflection type; there are finitely many such local templates at one
normalized step. Their iteration is already controlled by the permanent wrapping ideal rather than by a
sum over topological sectors. Taking the finite maximum proves the stated admission and leaves the joint
Haar–optical boundary-to-center mechanism unchanged.

E     Invariant tensor spaces through engineering dimension six
The low-dimensional color input can be stated without choosing a basis. The degree-two invariant
polynomial is unique for every simple g. A primitive degree-three invariant occurs only for Ar , r ≥ 2.
Hence the reflection-even dimension-four scalar block is one-dimensional for every G, while the dimension-
six block may contain the additional cubic curvature contraction in type A. The antisymmetric structure


                                                      111
tensor is always present through the bracket but contributes only inside the already retained covariant
derivative and commutator jets.
   The manuscript uses the complete finite quotient generated by curvature, covariant derivatives, metric
and epsilon contractions, invariant color tensors, total-difference classes, and equation-of-motion contacts
through the declared engineering cutoff. No argument relies on a hand-picked SU(2) basis once this
quotient is used.

F      Theorem-level specialization and dependency map
The purpose of this appendix is provenance rather than a new estimate. It identifies every independent
fixed-group input in the register CG , the first proof call at which that dependence enters, and the later
statements that consume it. In particular, the completion proof in Theorem 11.1 does not rely on an
unprinted constant or on a rank-one identity.

 Yang–Mills Existence, Mass Gap, and Nontriviality
for Fixed Compact Simple Gauge Groups via Karcher
      Blocking and Exact Haar–Pivot Matching
                                             Harold D. Kirk III1,2,∗
                         1
                             University of Memphis, Memphis, Tennessee 38152, USA
                                        2
                                            ORCID: 0009-0000-6014-8451

                                                August 3, 2026


                                                     Abstract
        Let G be a fixed compact connected simple Lie group and let ρ be a fixed faithful finite-dimensional
    unitary representation. We construct four-dimensional pure G Yang–Mills theory as a zero-spacing,
    infinite-volume limit of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures. The Wilson plaquette function
    is normalized at the identity to the background quadratic form used to define the coupling u = g 2 .
    A weighted forty-nine-path Karcher block, complete sixteen-alias normal section, charged chart-
    complement partition, and permanent rooted domain produce a uniform scale-two map on a centered
    invariant graph,
                                                 11 CA (G) log 2
                               u(2L) = u(L) +                    u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ).
                                                     24π 2

    The complete linear block certificate is independent of color and tensors with Ig ; all nonlinear
    constants are finite and may depend on G. At the physical handoff, a group-dependent product
    Sobolev order 2sG > dG /2 gives a Peter–Weyl Fourier–Wiener landing for the exact one-pivot and
    multipivot densities. Separated Haar pivots contract nonvacuum compact flux, optical covariance
    contracts normal-field stretches, and all remaining vertices retain their support charge. A single
    marked replica–BKAR forest then gives a boundary-to-center two-root connector and one physical
    clustering rate. General invariant-jet transport, displacement and stress Ward identities, finite
    dimension-four mixing, and a dimension-six rotation regulator yield a tempered, reflection-positive,
    translation-continuous, O(4)-covariant Schwinger family. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction gives a
    positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with unique vacuum and a strictly positive Hamiltonian
    gap. Endpoint-safe curvature-source transport produces a nonzero local vector with positive spectral
    mass in a finite interval above the gap. The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet
    observable theory. No estimate is asserted uniformly over the class of compact simple groups.

1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
1.1    Problem and result
The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
   Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
                                                        1
                                                                    
                                   wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
                                                       dρ
where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
   The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
                                              H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
                                            11 CA (G) log 2
                           u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
                                                 24π 2
with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
                                              dG                    dG
                                      sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
                                               4                      2
The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
but its constants may depend on CG .
Proposition 1.2 (Reflection-positive Wilson input). For every finite periodic lattice Λ and u > 0, in the
ordinary periodic link sector,
                                                "                    #
                             G,ρ

                          dµΛ,u (U ) = ZΛ,u exp −u  −1

is gauge invariant and reflection positive for coordinate reflections. Moreover wG,ρ ≥ 0, its zero set is the
identity, and on one fixed identity neighborhood
                                     cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2 .


                                                     3
Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
therefore applies.

1.3     Proof architecture
The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
   Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
                                                                                       Q
the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
   Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
   Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.
1.4     External inputs and exact proof boundary
Only the theorem-level inputs in Table 1 are imported. The fixed-group geometry, action charge, higher
Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
noncollapse source are internal.

f-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber
contraction are proved in the later normal-chart chapters.
Proposition 2.3 (Exact coarea–fiber identity). At finite volume, in the normal fiber chart,

                                    dU = J0 det(I + DN P )−1 dW dζ,

where the determinant is the ordinary finite-dimensional determinant. The fixed linear determinant is
owned once as a scalar normalization, and the field-dependent logarithm is one connected analytic activity.

Put g = u and Rg = g −2ε with 0 < ε < 1/6. The symbol χ > 0 denotes the curvature-bad threshold

   For an enlarged finite tube cell Q+ define the faithful fine Wilson charge and retained coherent deficit
by the same representation-normalized plaquette function used in the microscopic measure:
                                     X                              X

Let TQ be a fixed smooth invariant dispersion function formed from the squared relative-loop distances of
the forty-nine path inputs. It vanishes on the flat set, its small sublevel set lies inside the common Karcher
chart, and it uses only one fixed enlarged-cell template. On that sublevel set the retained holonomies
Wp′ are defined by the local Karcher atlas. Multiply the displayed retained deficit by a fixed chart cutoff
that is one on a prescribed smaller Karcher sublevel and zero before the edge of the larger Karcher tube;
this gives a smooth invariant extension to the complete finite-cell configuration space. The threshold tch
below is chosen so that the entire curvature-label support lies where this cutoff equals one.
Lemma 3.1 (Representation-normalized charge and derivative ledger). For every fixed G, faithful ρ,
and integer 0 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3, there are finite constants depending only on the fixed cell atlas such that

                                wG,ρ (U ) ≥ 0,      wG,ρ (U ) = 0 ⇐⇒ U = e,

                             cG ∥X∥2 ≤ wG,ρ (eX ) ≤ CG ∥X∥2       (∥X∥ ≤ r0,G ),
and
             ∥DwG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG wG,ρ (U )1/2 ,       ∥Dr wG,ρ (U )∥ ≤ CG,r     (2 ≤ r ≤ 2sG + 3).
The same bounds, with finite template constants, hold after composition with every Wilson word, retained
plaquette word, reflected word, or seam word used below.
Proof. Nonnegativity and the zero set are Proposition 1.2. In the chosen invariant metric the Hessian of
wG,ρ at the identity is positive definite; Taylor’s theorem gives the two-sided quadratic comparison. The
first-derivative estimate follows from that comparison on the identity tube. On its compact complement
wG,ρ has a positive minimum while its derivative is bounded, so the same inequality holds globally after
enlarging the constant. All higher derivatives are bounded because wG,ρ is a smooth matrix coefficient
on compact G. Every word in the fixed path atlas is a finite composition of multiplication and inversion,
so the chain rule gives the asserted finite derivative bounds.

Theorem 3.4 (Finite-cell retention and chart charge). There are constants Cret < ∞, cch > 0, and

                                            DQ (U ) ≤ Cret AQ+ (U )

                                   TQ (U ) ≥ tch /4     =⇒     AQ+ (U ) ≥ cch .

Proof. Use a boundary-rooted tree gauge on the fixed finite contractible cell. By Lemma 3.1, the zero set

inequality give
                                          AQ+ ≍ ∥dX∥2 ≍ ∥X∥2 ,

identity on the flat set give DQ + TQ ≤ C∥X∥2 . This proves the retention inequality on one neighborhood
of the flat set.
   Remove a smaller such neighborhood. On the resulting compact set, AQ+ has a strictly positive
minimum and the smoothly extended DQ is bounded, so their quotient is bounded there as well. This

bulk and aligned-reflection templates proves the stated uniformity.

trial lower bound control its normalized derivatives. Bell-polynomial differentiation of G−1  X preserves
only per-core polynomial/exponential losses, which are absorbed when multiplied by the bad numerator
estimate.

3.6    Irregular ownership and C 1 flatness
The irregular map must be assembled from the exact labelled density before any absolute-value estimate
is taken. This section fixes the ownership rule that was previously left as four composition hypotheses.
   Let X = {X1 , . . . , Xm } be a compatible family of bad cores. Compatibility means that the enlarged
integration cores I + (Xi ) are pairwise disjoint. Every pre-logarithmic local factor carries its actual finite
support; every exponentially local inverse, coarea, or Schur factor is first expanded in the permanent
exponential matrix algebra and each term is reported on its canonical connected hull.
Definition 3.22 (Canonical owner). For a reported connected factor A with support S(A) define
                               

                                 reg,    S(A) meets no integration core.
                               
                               

If a fixed-template boundary factor meets an integration core only through its prescribed ring, it is
assigned to that core precisely when its canonical root lies in I(Xi ); otherwise it remains regular. Ties
are therefore impossible.
Definition 3.23 (Gaussian/determinant allocation separation). For the exact joint fresh-detail law, let
H0 denote the finite-range ambient precision before the Karcher constraint and let Qopt be the actual
constrained precision of Theorem 4.9. Fix a separation collar RG . Cores closer than RG after their
ordinary interaction collars are merged into one core. A remaining compatible family satisfies:
(DAS1) every determinant endpoint whose two-jet enters the reference has an actual support of diameter
         at most the fixed number Rdet ;

         once;
(DAS3) all stagewise Gaussian conditionals are the chain-rule disintegration of one normalized joint law,
         with scalar normalization retained once at the exterior endpoint;
(DAS4) H0 is finite range, while Qopt and its inverse have uniform exponentially decaying kernels;
(DAS5) for distinct compatible cores,

leaves one compatible family. Enlarge RG until the displayed smallness holds.

4.3    Gaussian reference and optical covariance
This section constructs the Gaussian objects used by the weak block, Wick coordinates, replica forests,
and source connectors. Two objects must not be conflated:
1. the fixed tangent Feynman Gaussian on mean-zero lattice one-forms;
2. its exact conditional detail Gaussian for the linearized forty-nine-path Karcher block.
The first carries the massless retained field. The second is an optical, high-pass covariance with a uniform
gap. The nonlinear Wilson action, Haar and exponential-coordinate density, Karcher coarea factor, cutoffs,
inherited interaction, and source factors are not hidden in either covariance; they are exposed as exact
activities after the normal-fiber change of variables.

                           Bj Jj = I,        Bj Cjd = 0,       C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ .

4.3.3 Exact Gaussian disintegration and harmonic extension
Let C≥j be the mean-zero Feynman covariance before the scale-j block and let Bj = 12 Af,j be the
normalized linearized retained map. On the mean-zero coarse space define

                                 Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,        Jj = C≥j Bj∗ (Cjc )−1 ,

and
                                              Cjd = C≥j − Jj Cjc Jj∗ .
All inverses are taken only on the stated mean-zero spaces; torons remain in the retained compact
background.
Theorem 4.11 (Exact linear Gaussian fiber decomposition). The operators above satisfy

                           Bj Jj = I,        Bj Cjd = 0,       C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ .

If W ∼ N (0, Cjc ) and ζ ∼ N (0, Cjd ) are independent, then

                                                  A = Jj W + ζ

has covariance C≥j and Bj A = W . Conversely, this is the exact conditional disintegration of the fine
Feynman Gaussian given the retained linear block. In the folded Fourier frame, Cjd is the pushforward of
Copt from Corollary 4.10.
Proof. These are the finite-dimensional Gaussian Schur-complement identities. Since Cjc = Bj C≥j Bj∗ ,
Bj Jj = I. Substitution gives Bj Cjd = 0 and the covariance sum. The characteristic function of Jj W + ζ is
therefore the one with covariance C≥j . The folded optical parameterization T (k) identifies the conditional
detail space with ker Af (k), and the conditional precision is the restriction of Hf to that space.


                                                        28
  After canonical scale-two rescaling, define C≥j+1 to be the rescaled coarse covariance Cjc . Thus the
notation
                                          C≥j = Cj + C≥j+1
used below means the exact identity of Theorem 4.11, with Cj = Cjd and with the remaining covariance
embedded by Jj before the canonical identification. It is not a declaration of an unconstructed translation-
invariant product slicing.
Corollary 4.12 (Harmonic-extension Wick martingale). For every finite polynomial P in the fine tangent
field,
                           Eζ∼Cjd : P (Jj W + ζ) :C≥j =: (P ◦ Jj )(W ) :Cjc .
                                                       

The identity commutes with every fixed background mark and every prescribed finite family of passive
bounded source marks. The contraction operators commute and C≥j = Cjd + Jj Cjc Jj∗ . Therefore
                              1
                              2 ∆C d       − 21 ∆C≥j                    − 21 ∆C c
                          e       j    e               P (Jj W + ·) 0 = e      j    (P ◦ Jj )(W ).

The covariance is fixed and independent of the background and source parameters, so those derivatives
commute with the contraction operators.
4.3.4 Exact Wilson–Haar factorization in the nonlinear fiber chart
Let PN (W, ζ) be the exact nonlinear normal-fiber chart proved in Sections 2.4– 3.2, and let Jcoarea be its
exact finite-volume coarea density. On the inner regular chart, write dγj (ζ) for the normalized Gaussian
with covariance Cjd .
Theorem 4.13 (Exact regular-reference identity). At finite volume and before any thermodynamic or
cutoff limit, the inner regular part of one Wilson fiber integral has the exact form
                Z                          Z              Z
                            −1
                   F (U )e−u SW (U ) dU = dW ρ0,j (W ) dγj (ζ) F (PN (W, ζ))e−Vj (W,ζ;u) .

Here ρ0,j owns the field-independent linear Jacobian and the empty support Gaussian normalization
exactly once, while
                                                             1
                       Vj (W, ζ; u) = u−1 SW (PN (W, ζ)) − ⟨ζ, (Cjd )−1 ζ⟩
                                                             2
                                      − log JHaar (W, ζ) − log Jcoarea (W, ζ) − cj (W )

is defined with cj (W ) chosen to remove the empty-support term. Thus the identity is a change of measure,
not a Gaussian approximation. All nonquadratic Wilson terms, the complete field-dependent Haar and
coarea factors, normal-section corrections, inherited interactions, sources, and regular cutoffs remain
visible in Vj or in the charged irregular operators. No Gaussian product factorization across spatial
components is asserted.
Proof. Apply the exact finite-volume normal-fiber and coarea identity to the compact Wilson integral in
the boundary-rooted gauge. Multiply and divide by the strictly positive normalized density of γj . The
displayed formula then follows by the definition of Vj and the once-only normalization cj . The exact
partition into inner, curvature, collar, and outer labels is made before logarithms and is recombined
before reflection positivity is used. Gauge covariance, signed-hypercubic covariance, and aligned-reflection
compatibility follow from covariance of the Karcher map, its normal section, the tree gauge, and the
scalar Feynman precision. Individual artificial Gaussian detail measures are not claimed to be reflection
positive.
Corollary 4.14 (Uniform connector row). Let X, Y be two finite scale-j supports. Every covariance
contraction from the exact detail Gaussian is bounded by the scale-local bond
                                                 XX

                                             4                         8
and
                                                    1

Consequently the block correction raises engineering degree by an even positive amount. It has no

Proof. For the diagonal entry,
                                                                                
