                               Nµµ (k) = cos(kµ /2) ϑ + (1 − ϑ)           cos kλ  .
                                                                   λ̸=µ

It is even in every variable, so all first and third total derivatives at zero vanish. Direct differentiation
gives ∂µ2 Nµµ (0) = −1/4 and, for λ ̸= µ, ∂λ2 Nµµ (0) = −(1 − ϑ) = −1/8. For µ ̸= ν,
                            1−ϑ
                  Nµν (k) =       sin kµ sin(kν /2) [2 cos kα cos kβ + cos kα + cos kβ + 2] .
                              6
The bracket equals six at zero, so ∂µ ∂ν Nµν (0) = (1 − ϑ) · (1/2) = 1/16. Its Taylor series begins in total
degree two and the next terms have total degree four, hence every total-degree-three derivative vanishes.
These formulas also show that every correction raises engineering degree by an even positive amount.

Stable jet matrix. Order the stable Wick-local jet fiber by increasing declared engineering degree:
                                           [2]
                                         Jst = J5 ⊕ J6 ⊕ J7 ⊕ J8 .
Theorem 4.17 (Stable jet-matrix theorem). At the Gaussian point, the one-step stable derivative is
                                          Ljet = Πirr S2 Γ(J) Πirr ,
where Γ(J) is Gaussian second quantization of the harmonic extension and S2 is canonical scale-two
rescaling.
   In the displayed degree ordering,
                                        2 I5    0             0

after combining all allowed degree-+2r entries into the finite blocks B∆,∆+2r . More generally the matrix
is block upper triangular and
                                           gr∆ Ljet = 24−∆ I.
   All entries are finite and uniform under the standing convention: they are derivatives at the flat point
of one fixed finite forty-nine-path cell and its fixed finite Wick conversion.

and on the unmarked scalar subfiber,
                                                             1

Proof. The Wick martingale eliminates every covariance-contraction lowering term. Lemma 4.16 gives
identity on the same-degree field jet and only degree-+2r corrections. Canonical cell rescaling multiplies
a homogeneous degree-∆ jet by 24−∆ .
   The extraction projector removes every declared jet below the stable threshold. Hence the stable matrix
is block upper triangular with the displayed diagonal. Its entries come from finitely many derivatives of
finite local analytic maps, so they are finite uniform constants. The spectrum of a finite block triangular
matrix is the union of the spectra of its diagonal blocks.

4.5    Localization and exact collect
Fix one finite dyadic macrostep L = 2s . All fields are expressed in the scale-dependent Wick coordinates
of the stable-jet theorem. Engineering degree includes discrete derivatives and every declared active
structural mark.

Rooted histories and collect. Let HL be the absolutely summable family of connected regular
macrostep histories. A history h has a reported connected support P (h), a distinguished root, and a
canonical hull
                                             Y = H(h).
The hull contains the reported supports, shortest covariance connector paths, and the fixed finite atlas
collar.
Definition 4.18 (Exact collect). For a coarse connected support Y , define
                                                     X
                                        (CL F )Y =         Fh .
                                                       h∈HL
                                                      H(h)=Y

The history certificate is retained as a passive label until this sum is formed.
Lemma 4.19 (Collect is a rooted partition). The sets

                                              {h : H(h) = Y }

partition HL . Hence collect is exact, and for every bounded linear localization operator AY ,
                                                       X
                                       AY (CL F )Y =        AY Fh .
                                                      H(h)=Y

No history is omitted or counted twice.
Proof. The canonical hull and history certificate are deterministic functions of the reported history.
Absolute convergence in the rooted activity norm permits bounded linear operations to pass through the
history sum.

Gauge-covariant local jet projector. For a rooted support Y , choose the canonical root cell r(Y ).
Parallel-transport every local tensor to r(Y ) along the canonical hull tree and write the resulting local
field in the finite normal-coordinate atlas.
    Let JD (Y ) be the finite-dimensional space of gauge-complete, signed-hypercubic covariant, Wick-local
jets of declared engineering degree at most D, including the extraction sector and the stable jet sector.
Lemma 4.20 (Discrete covariant localization). There is a projector

                                          LocD,Y : FY 7−→ JD (Y )

with the following properties.
1. Loc2D,Y = LocD,Y , and it is exact on JD (Y ).
2. It commutes with gauge transformations, signed-hypercubic symmetries, and aligned reflection.
3. For 0 ≤ m ≤ 3,
                                ∥ LocD,Y FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD ∥FY ∥[m] .
4. If LocD,Y FY = 0, then after canonical scale-L rescaling,

                                 ∥TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

  The estimate includes both the nonlocal output and its newly generated local projection:

                ∥ LocD TL FY ∥[m] + ∥(1 − LocD )TL FY ∥[m] ≤ CD (1 + τ (Y ))pD L3−D ∥FY ∥[m] .

Proof. On each finite local template, take the gauge-complete Taylor expansion in normal coordinates
and scale-dependent Wick monomials, transport all coefficients to the canonical root, and average over
the finite stabilizer. The dual coefficient functionals use finitely many derivatives and finite-difference


                                                     32
moments. Transport along a tree of radius τ (Y ) costs at most a polynomial in 1 + τ (Y ), proving the
third row.
   If LocD,Y FY = 0, every declared Taylor coefficient through degree D vanishes. The integral form of
the covariant Taylor remainder begins at degree D + 1. Canonical rescaling gives one root-preimage factor
L4 and L−(D+1) from the smallest omitted engineering degree. Thus the gain is

                                              L4−(D+1) = L3−D .

The regular linearized block kernel is analytic on the fixed tube and bounded through the three active
marked rows, so integration and the local coefficient functionals preserve this remainder estimate. Applying
the projector after integration proves the displayed localization-commutator bound; in particular, a
remainder cannot feed the finite local block without paying the same L3−D factor.

   In particular, the macrostep envelope LΓ does not multiply the finite local diagonal.
Proof. On the finite local Wick-jet quotient, Gaussian integration is exact second quantization and
                                         [s]
canonical rescaling gives the matrix Ljet proved in Theorem 4.17. This is the local-to-local block.
   For every coordinate containing a localization remainder, the preceding lemma gives the L3−D gain on
short supports. Local jets that produce degree above D also lie in the same remainder because the exact
forty-nine-path symbol raises degree only by even positive amounts. Thus all off-diagonal local/remainder
blocks belong to NL , while the finite

ore commute with the contraction operators. Wick contraction is applied to the complete gauge-

Logarithmic support dichotomy. Assume the exact summed regular macrostep envelope
                                        [m]

where τL is the rooted coarse tree radius.

Theorem 4.22 (Exact Loc/collect dichotomy). For every 0 ≤ m ≤ 3, the nonlocal block NL splits exactly
into long and short rows:
                                      NL = NLlong + NLshort .
  The long row satisfies
                                             ∥NLlong ∥[m] ≤ Cm L−3 .

  The short row satisfies

                                  ∥NLshort ∥[m] ≤ CD,m (1 + R(L))pD LΓ−(D−3) .

  Equivalently,
                                              δD = D − 3 −→ ∞.
   The estimates are uniform in periodic volume and preceding weak depth. They remain valid with one
or two passive exterior roots and every fixed finite passive source order.


                                                        33
Proof. For the long sector,
                                              [m]                                               [m]
                                   X                                            X
                         sup                  cL (Y ) ≤ e−κR(L) sup                    eκτL (Y ) cL (Y )
                          b                                                b
                                    Y ∋b                                        Y ∋b
                               τL (Y )>R(L)

                                                        ≤ Cm LΓ e−κR(L) ≤ Cm L−3 .

   For the short sector, first apply exact collect and then the local projector. Linearity and absolute
convergence make this identical to localizing each history and collecting by its canonical hull. Every
stable remainder lies in the kernel of LocD , while every off-diagonal local/remainder block has the same
Taylor-remainder gain. Since
                                              τL (Y ) ≤ R(L),
the discrete covariant localization lemma gives

                                              CD,m (1 + R(L))pD L3−D

per collected activity. Summing the rooted envelope contributes LΓ , proving the short bound.
   The canonical root and history certificate are selected once, so neither hull reassignment nor collect
produces a volume factor. Uniformity follows from the rooted envelope and the finite local template
atlas.

Boundaries and aligned reflection. Use the corrected reflection schedule:
• block the two closed half-spaces separately;
• retain the reflection-plane link layer as fixed boundary data;
• use only path templates with longitudinal extent [0, 2] and transverse extent [−1, 1]3 relative to their
  anchor.
Theorem 4.23 (Boundary-template Loc dichotomy). For fixed D, L, the bulk and aligned-reflection
localization atlas has finitely many rooted template classes. The preceding projector, commutator, long-row,
and short-row estimates hold on each class with one maximum constant independent of volume.
   The exact weak/irregular partition assigns every moving-cutoff derivative to the charged irregular row
before reflection positivity is invoked.
Proof. The corrected half-space schedule prevents a retained path from coupling the two open half-spaces
except through the fixed plane layer. The stated path extents and the finite radius R(L) give only finitely
many rooted boundary intersections up to translation and the aligned-reflection stabilizer. Construct
LocD templatewise and average over that stabilizer. Taking the maximum of finitely many constants
proves the uniform estimate.
   The labelled weak/collar/outer densities recombine pointwise before the Osterwalder–Schrader form is
formed, so localization does not assert reflection positivity of an individual slice or label.

   (i) principal same-degree transport and its inverse grow by at most a fixed power of the running

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix kernel and first three marked derivatives are exponentially local, uniformly
in volume.
Proof. Every path has the same endpoints, and the weighted squared-distance functional is invariant
under simultaneous left and right multiplication. Uniqueness of the Karcher minimizer gives gauge and
orientation covariance. The signed-corner path family is invariant under the lattice symmetry group.
At the flat point, the exact Fourier symbol is the color-free spacetime symbol tensored with Ig and has
acoustic value 2I ⊗ Ig and a uniformly gapped normal frame. The full-alias section and normal-fiber
contraction are proved in the later normal-chart chapters.
Proposition 2.3 (Exact coarea–fiber identity). At finite volume, in the normal fiber chart,

                                    dU = J0 det(I + DN P )−1 dW dζ,


                                                     5
where the determinant is the ordinary finite-dimensional determinant. The fixed linear determinant is
owned once as a scalar normalization, and the field-dependent logarithm is one connected analytic activity.

2.2    Exact normal fiber chart and coarea
Let Xm , Ym be exponentially weighted Banach coordinate spaces and let

                    L : Xm → Ym ,         P : Ym → Xm ,              LP = I,        ∥P ∥m = p < 1.

Put K = ker L and write

                            B(x) = Lx + N (

=== macro-cell boundaries hit 1 at 242685 ===
Reserve respectively mref − a′ref , areg − a′reg , and airr − a′irr in the three input rows. After multiplying
by the pivot charge eλ|IP (A)| , the reserved exponent absorbs that charge for sufficiently large finite R
and yields exactly the three terms of ∆R (u). Thus no diameter estimate is used to pay a spanning-tree
weight. Coordinate substitutions on inactive paths are independent of all live pivots and cannot create
additional pivot incidence. Physical-source marks of order at most two change only Cλ ; the independent
pivot derivatives through order 2sG are already included in the three norms in (HT5).
   Finally, on an active path no anchor is reconstructed: all path links remain original variables. Splitting
the ordered path product immediately gives (49). Haar invariance therefore annihilates the Haar part of
every nontrivial carrier at an uncovered pivot under the same integral in which the product reference was
obtained.

Corollary 6.11 (Correct one-pivot minorization). Every one-pivot reference satisfies

                                                          νi,η ≥ ϵ⋆ mG ,

and its centered Peter–Weyl operator has norm at most θ⋆ . The constant may be exponentially small in
1/ū; only strict positivity, not weak-coupling smallness, is consumed. A normalized density whose log
oscillation is at most Ω⋆ is bounded below by e−Ω⋆ times Haar. Split off that Haar part.

6.4    Finite-volume Perron input
Proposition 6.13 (Finite-volume Perron input from two-scale landing). For every finite spatial volume,
the one-step matching transfer kernel relative to product Haar is continuous, symmetric, and strictly
positive. If MΛ is the finite number of one-link updates in a sweep, then
                                                          −1
                                          KL (x, y) ≥ ϵM
                                                       ⋆ ZΛ,L > 0.
                                                        Λ



The compact transfer operator has a simple maximal eigenvalue and a unique strictly positive Perron
eigenfunction. No volume-uniform lower bound for the complete kernel is asserted.
Proof. Apply Theorem 6.10 with one live pivot at a time. Corollary 6.11 gives the finite-volume product
lower bound. The aligned half-step is SL∗ SL , so the transfer is positive and self-adjoint. Jentzsch–Perron
applies in finite volume.

6.5    Exact Haar–optical matching law
The matching macroblock used after the compact first crossing is the exact pushforward by oriented
length-L path products. It is useful to distinguish the coordinates that define the map from the coordinates
used to estimate a particular retained coefficient.
   Fix one proof color. Its straight macro-paths are pairwise edge-disjoint and have mutually disjoint
buffered interiors. For one path γ = (e1 , . . . , eL ) define
                                            Pγ (U ) = Ue1 · · · UeL .
The retained macro-link is Wγ = Pγ (U ). For paths not carrying an active coefficient one may use the
standard coordinates
                            Wγ = Pγ (U ),      Zγ,k = Uek , 2 ≤ k ≤ L,
with
                                         Ue1 = Wγ (Zγ,2 · · · Zγ,L )−1 .
The full map BL is the finite composition of the colorwise pushforwards, with the reflection plane on
macro-cell boundaries.
Lemma 6.14 (Unit-Haar macroblock disintegration). For every path,
                                L
                                Y                              L
                                                               Y
                                      dmG (Uek ) = dmG (Wγ )         dmG (Zγ,k ).
                                k=1                            k=2

Consequently the simultaneous path-product map has unit Haar Jacobian, is gauge covariant at path
endpoints, and intertwines aligned reflection.
Proof. For fixed details, Ue1 7→ Wγ is right multiplication by a fixed group element. Haar invariance
proves the identity. Edge-disjointness tensorizes it, gauge transformations telescope, and reflection reverses
and inverts the product.
Definition 6.15 (Carrier-adapted hybrid chart). Let A be the finite set of retained macro-links carrying
nontrivial Peter–Weyl labels, bounded probes, or retained-source derivatives in the coefficient under study.
On every path γ ∈ A retain the original link coordinates
                                         Xγ,k = Uek ,        1 ≤ k ≤ L,
and regard Wγ = Pγ (X) only as the output map. On paths γ ∈        / A use the standard (Wγ , Zγ,2:L )
coordinates. Choose live pivots only among the X variables on active paths.

Conditioning on all variables except separated live pivots on the active paths leaves the exact product Haar

Proof. Use the identity of Lemma 6.14 only on inactive paths and the identity map on active paths.

Lemma 6.17 (Carrier incidence and original-support locality). Let p be a live pivot on an active path γ.
With all other active path links fixed,
                                        Wγ = Bp Xp±1 Cp ,                                           (51)
where Bp , Cp are independent of Xp . If a connected physical activity with original reported support Y
meets k separated live pivots, then
                                          τ (Y ) ≥ cp R(k − 1).                                     (52)
Anchor reconstruction on an inactive path cannot alter the activity’s live pivot incidence.
Proof. Equation (51) is the ordered product split before and after p. The second statement follows by
joining the k pivot buffers inside the connected reported support. Every inactive-path coordinate is a
function only of links on that inactive path; because no live pivot lies there, its substitution cannot create
dependence on a live pivot.

Corollary 6.19 (Definition, exactness, and coefficientwise use of BL ). The matching macroblock BL is
a well-defined finite exact pushforward on the compact first-crossing class. For every Fourier, source-
marked, or normalized-probe coefficient of its retained density, the active paths may be analyzed in the
carrier-adapted hybrid chart without changing that coefficient. The charged pivot expansion uses precisely
the reference of Lemma 6.18; gauge covariance, reflection, and dyadic composition remain properties of
the underlying exact pushforward.
6.5.1 Compact collect with optical field live
The matching theorem is now proved on one exact joint law, before the normal/optical variables are
marginalized. This is the decisive ordering. The compact retained variables are reduced conditionally by
Haar–pivot integration; the resulting activities remain functions of the complete optical field; and only
then is the connected logarithm taken by the marked Gaussian forest theorem. Thus no retained density,
latent mixture, or residual-only gas is inserted between the physical law and the connected source kernel.



                                                       59
6.5.2 One exact joint matching law
Let W denote the compact retained variables in the exact Karcher chart and let ζ denote its complete
optical normal field. At finite volume and with physical source J, Theorem 4.13, Theorem 3.28, and the
exact owner map give
                              dµJ (W, ζ) = Z(J)−1 dγopt (ζ) dH(W ) e−VJ (W,ζ) .                   (53)
Here dH(W ) is product normalized Haar on the compact retained links. Indeed, ρ0,j in Theorem 4.13 owns
only the field-independent linear Jacobian and empty-support normalization; Proposition 6.8 absorbs that
scalar into Z(J) and restores product Haar. Every nonempty retained reference factor remains explicit in
VJ . Every nonempty Haar, coarea, chart, determinant, Schur, regular, irregular, seam, wrapping, and
source occurrence belongs to exactly one factor of VJ . Every scalar depending on an unintegrated variable
remains inside (53). Define only the complete normalized source logarithm

                                          W(J) = log Z(J) − log Z(0).                                   (54)

No marginal density is logarithmized.

                         νp,η = ϵ⋆ mG + θ⋆ νep,η ,       0 < ϵ⋆ < 1,      θ⋆ = 1 − ϵ⋆ < 1.

                        max ∥Tp,η Qp ∥L∞ →L∞ , ∥Tp,η Qp ∥L2 (mG )→L2 (mG ) ≤ θ⋆ .                       (55)

This is the complete nonvacuum projection estimate, not a coefficientwise claim. It is group-generic.
Source marks are inserted later as marked vertices and may remove at most one unmarked payment per

6.5.3 Final compact Haar-pivot collect
In (53), write U for the compact matching links and keep the complete optical field ζ and the actual
local source roots unchanged. Apply the exact compact-only path-product block BL to U as a finite
matching operation. Corollary 6.19 permits coefficientwise use of its hybrid chart, while every compact
path detail is integrated in the same complete joint partition function. No optical variable is identified
with a compact path detail, and no source is assumed fiber-constant under BL . The block is used once to
place many same-law Haar pivots inside each coarse compact edge.
Definition 6.21 (Matching-stage tri-graded norm). For a compact-stage activity family F = (FX ) and
fixed positive mark weights σpiv , σζ , σJ , set
                                                                  |α|

Here Dpiv differentiates only the declared live original-link pivot variables. The derivative Dζ is a Fréchet
derivative in the unintegrated optical coordinate at the compact-collect call site, and DJ differentiates
the physical source. The three indices are independent; no optical derivative is counted as a structural
pivot derivative or as a source leaf. The pivot order 2sG is the fixed-group Sobolev order required by the
product Peter–Weyl landing; the optical order three and the physical-source order two are unchanged
from the permanent marked hierarchy.
